Compute gradient efficiently:
```
def grad_weighted(p, weights, n):
    grad = np.zeros(n)
    # only non-zero weights; weights length 2n-1
    for idx,w in enumerate(weights): if w>thresh:
       k=idx
       lo=max(0,k-n+1); hi=min(n-1,k)
       grad[lo:hi+1] += w * 2.0 * p[k-hi:k-lo+1][::-1]
    return 2.0*n*grad
```

Detailed mirror:
```
def soft_mirror_step(p, n, K, tau_frac, eta, beta, rng=None, momentum=None):
    c = fft_conv_self(p)
    c = np.maximum(c,0)
    M = c.max()
    F = 2.0*n*M
    top_idx = np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:] if K < len(c) else np.arange(len(c))
    top_c = c[top_idx]
    if tau_frac <=0:
       # uniform among near maxima? maybe top c equal within 1e-9
       thresh = M - max(1e-12, 1e-6*M)
       active = top_c >= thresh
       if not active.any(): active[top_c.argmax()]=True
       w = np.zeros(K); w[active]=1/active.sum()
    else:
       tau = max(1e-300, tau_frac*M)
       ex = (top_c - M)/tau
       ex = np.clip(ex, -50, 0)
       w = np.exp(ex)
       s=w.sum()
       if s<=0: w = np.full(K,1/K)
       else: w/=s
    grad = np.zeros(n)
    # To speed, sort top_idx maybe by descending c? no matter
    for k,wk in zip(top_idx,w):
       if wk <= 0: continue
       lo=max(0, int(k)-n+1); hi=min(n-1,int(k))
       if lo<=hi:
           grad[lo:hi+1] += wk * 2.0 * p[int(k)-hi:int(k)-lo+1][::-1]
    grad *= 2.0*n
    # normalize gradient? eta scales. Could clip grad to avoid overflow.
    gmax=np.abs(grad).max()
    if gmax>0: grad = grad * (1.0/gmax) maybe? If normalize, eta becomes relative. Better not normalize entirely because constant component removed by softmax; if constant, no step. But if we clip magnitude to e.g 10, eta in gradient units.
```
If we normalize grad to max 1, eta can be dimensionless. Since softmax subtracts mean, only differences matter. Normalize by gmax to stabilize. But if gradient all constant (uniform), gmax>0 but differences zero; z update constant, p unchanged. Good. Use normalized grad `gn = grad / max(1e-12, gmax)`. Then eta can be, say, 0.1-2.0.
- momentum: `v = beta*v + (1-beta)*gn`? Momentum should accumulate gradient direction; if using normalized, okay. But if gradient direction changes, heavy ball. Use v = β*v + gn? Then v maybe larger. Simpler: v = β*v + (1-β)*gn; step z -= η*v/(1-β)? Or use v = β*v + gn; step η*gn? Let's implement Adam-like? Mirror + Adam might be good.
   Maintain m,v for normalized grad. Update z -= η * m_hat/(sqrt(v_hat)+eps). But z update with Adam adapts per coordinate; can be effective. However mirror with Adam may break simple. Let's consider.
- Since p=softmax(z), and z can be updated with Adam on grad_p. Use Adam in z-space:
   grad_p normalized. m=β1 m+(1-β1) g; v=β2 v+(1-β2)g^2; z= z - lr * m_hat/(sqrt(v_hat)+eps). Then p=softmax(z). This is like mirror with adaptive steps. Might be robust. lr dimensionless e.g 0.5, decay.
   But z has large values; okay. We need maintain z initialized log(p). When normalize p, we should reset z to log(p) to avoid drift? We can maintain z as current log unnormalized. After p=softmax(z), we can set z = np.log(p) (subtract log sum). This discards absolute scale. Good.

Potential issue: Adam with normalized gradient may take too large steps, causing p extremes. Use small lr and backtracking? We can do backtracking for lr: try current lr, if F not decreased, reduce? Computing multiple F expensive. But we can monitor failure and reduce.

Algorithm for a run:
```
def run_soft_mirror(p0, n, time_budget, rng, initial_temp, final_temp, K, lr_init, lr_decay, beta1, beta2, perturb_prob):
    p = normalize(p0)
    z = np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-300))
    m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n); t=0
    best=F; best_p=p.copy()
    temp=initial_temp
    while time<:
       # compute c, top, w, grad as above
       F=2n*M
       if F<best: update
       # update temp: linear/exponential to final based on t or time
       g = grad / gmax
       Adam update z, p=softmax(z)
       t+=1
       # occasional perturb: z += rng.normal(0, noise, n); p=softmax(z); maybe with schedule
       # if no improvement for many iterations, perturb or reduce K? 
    return best_p, best
```

However, this does not ensure monotonic descent. We can add simple backtracking per iteration: After computing proposed p_new, evaluate F_new (which we already computed? We computed F at old p before update. To get F_new need another FFT. That's expensive (3x). But maybe we can accept non-guaranteed with annealing. But to polish, we need decrease. We can use step size small enough. Or do one extra FFT to evaluate new p and if F_new worse, keep old and reduce lr. That doubles cost but ensures no increase (plus noise). At 2400s, okay. We can do backtracking with maybe one trial. If new worse, reduce lr by factor 0.5 and retry with smaller (maybe up to 3). Each retry requires new p from z - smaller step and one FFT. Could be okay.

But note gradient computed at old p; for smaller step, same gradient okay. So:
- Compute old c, M, F0, grad (top loop). Save.
- Try step with current lr (maybe adapt): z_trial = z - lr_eff * adam_step? (not normalized?); p_trial = softmax. Evaluate F1. If F1 < F0 (or <= F0 + tol), accept and set lr = lr*1.5 maybe. Else reduce lr*0.5 and retry (maybe up to 2-3). If all fail, keep old, reduce lr.
This will be slower but more robust. For initial exploration with fixed decaying no backtracking maybe. We can implement two modes: `adaptive=True` with line search for polish.

But in Adam, the update depends on m/v; if we reject step, should we still update m/v? Usually yes based on gradient at current point, or not? We can update m/v before trial; if reject, keep but okay.

Need compute F_new often. We can cache c? If accept, c_new can be used for next iteration? The next iteration needs top gradient at p_new; we already have p_new but not c_new specifically (we evaluated M only; we might have full c_new from FFT if we keep). So we can return c_new to avoid recomputing old self conv next. But top gradient requires c_new weights. In loop:
   current p, current c (computed). Compute F and grad.
   Trial p_new; compute c_new, F_new.
   If accept: p=p_new; c=c_new.
   If reject: keep p,c.
This uses one FFT for trial. Next iteration uses c current (already computed). So one FFT per accepted/rejected trial plus maybe extra retries. Good.

But gradient top loop uses c current; no FFT. Great.

Let's design functions:

```
def normalize_p(a, n=None):
   a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
   a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
   s=a.sum()
   if s<1e-15: return np.full(n,1/n)
   return a/s
```

`fft_conv_self(p, fsize=None)`:
```
n=len(p)
size=2*n-1
if fsize is None: fsize=1 << (size-1).bit_length()
P=fft(p, fsize)
c=ifft(P*P).real
c[:size]=np.maximum(c[:size],0.0)
return c[:size]
```
Because p positive, c should positive; numerical neg clip.

`compute_top_gradient(p, c, K, tau_frac, scale=2.0*n)`:
```
L=len(c)
K=min(K,L)
if K==L: top=np.arange(L)
else: top=np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:]  # not sorted; okay
top_c=c[top]
M=top_c.max() (or c.max)
if tau_frac <= 0:
   thresh = M - max(1e-14, 1e-8*M) # very close
   active = top_c >= thresh
   if active.sum()==0: active = top_c == np.amax(top_c) maybe
   w = np.where(active,1,0); w/=w.sum()
else:
   tau=max(1e-300, tau_frac*M)
   ex=(top_c-M)/tau
   ex=np.clip(ex,-40,0)
   w=np.exp(ex)
   s=w.sum()
   if s == 0 or not finite:
       # fallback: largest c gets weight 1
       w=np.zeros(K); w[int(np.argmax(top_c))]=1
   else: w/=s
grad=np.zeros(n)
# maybe sort top_idx by descending w? no
for k,wk in zip(top,w):
    if wk <= 1e-15: continue
    k=int(k)
    lo = 0 if k < n-1 else k-n+1
    hi = n-1 if k > n-1 else k
    # p indices j=k-i for i in lo..hi -> j from k-lo down to k-hi? Let's verify previous: i=lo -> j=k-lo; i=hi -> j=k-hi. segment lo..hi gets p[j] reversed (j descending)
    grad[lo:hi+1] += wk * 2.0 * p[k-hi:k-lo+1][::-1]
grad *= 2.0*n
return grad, M
```
Need ensure p slice indices correct: Example n=3, k=1, lo=0,hi=1. p[k-hi:k-lo+1]=p[0:2] `[p0,p1]`, reversed `[p1,p0]`. grad[0]+=2w p1, grad[1]+=2w p0. Derivative c1=2p0p1: ∂/∂p0=2p1, ∂/∂p1=2p0 yes. k=2 (center n-1? n=3 k=2), lo=0,hi=2. p[0:3][::-1]=[p2,p1,p0]; c2=2p0p2+p1^2; derivative grad [2w p2,2w p1,2w p0] yes. k=3, lo=1,hi=2. p[1:3][::-1]=[p2,p1]; c3=2p1p2; derivative grad[1]=2p2, grad[2]=2p1 yes. Good.

When top K using argpartition unsorted, w correspond. Good.

Gradient normalization: `gmax = np.abs(grad).max(); if gmax>0: grad = grad/gmax`. But if all constant (uniform), gmax>0; normalized constant => softmax no change. Good.

`softmax(z)`: `z = z - z.max(); expz=np.exp(z); p=expz/expz.sum()`.

Backtracking function:
```
def mirror_optimize(p0, n, max_time, rng, start_tau=0.2, end_tau=0.001, K=32, lr=0.3, max_retries=3, beta_mom=0.0, momentum=False):
    p=normalize_p(p0)
    z=np.log(p + 1e-300); z-=z.mean()
    c=fft_conv_self(p)
    F=2n*c.max()
    best=F; best_p=p.copy()
    v=np.zeros(n)
    lr_cur=lr
    no_improve=0
    start=time.monotonic(); T_total=max_time
    while time - start < max_time-2:
       progress=(time-start)/T_total
       tau = start_tau + (end_tau-start_tau)*progress (or exponential)
       grad, M = compute_top_gradient(p,c,K,tau)
       F=2n*M
       if F < best - 1e-12:
           best=F; best_p=p.copy(); no_improve=0
       else: no_improve+=1
       g=grad
       gmax=...; g/=gmax
       if momentum: v = beta*v + (1-beta)*g  # maybe effective?
       stepg = v if momentum else g
       # clip stepg to [-step_cap, step_cap]
       stepg = np.clip(stepg, -5, 5)
       # line search
       trial_lr=lr_cur
       accepted=False
       for r in range(max_retries):
           z_trial = z - trial_lr*stepg
           z_trial -= z_trial.mean()
           p_trial = softmax(z_trial)
           c_trial = fft_conv_self(p_trial)
           F_trial = 2n*c_trial.max()
           # also maybe check no NaN
           if np.isfinite(F_trial) and F_trial <= F + 1e-12:
               z=z_trial; p=p_trial; c=c_trial; F=F_trial
               accepted=True
               if F_trial < F0? maybe increase lr
               break
           trial_lr *= 0.5
       if not accepted:
           # keep, reduce lr more
           lr_cur *= 0.5
       else:
           if no_accept_count>... reset lr? Maybe adapt.
       # occasional perturb if no improve X
       if no_improve > 2000 and rng...:
           # add noise to z relative to p? z += rng.normal(0, noise)
```

But line search using same trial_lr after retry: If first trial worse, we reduce, but with momentum step maybe. If accept, we can increase lr up to cap (e.g 2.0) if F decreases significantly. But if F just equal, keep. Use:

```
decreased = F_trial < F - tol
if accepted:
   if decreased: lr_cur = min(lr_max, lr_cur*1.1)
   else: lr_cur = lr_cur*0.8
else: lr_cur *=0.5
```

Reverse -> h[2n-2],...,h[n-1] = m0..m_{n-1}. So `m = h[n-1 : 2*n-1][::-1]` (since L-1=2n-2). Good. grad = 2*2n*m? m = weighted sum ∑ p_j w_{i+j}; ∂F/∂p_i=2n * 2*m_i.

So full gradient via FFT:
```
def full_soft_gradient(p, weights, n): # weights sum1 length L
   w_rev = weights[::-1]
   h = fft_convolve(p,w_rev) # custom linear convolve two arrays via fft
   m = h[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]
   grad = 4.0*n*m
```

Let's design a smooth optimization loop:
- Choose τ schedule from e.g 0.1 down to 1e-5 M (absolute? We can use temp = temp_frac * current_M). At each iteration, compute smooth objective `Fsmooth = 2n*(M + τ*logsumexp((c-M)/τ))` at current p. The hard objective `Fhard=2n*M`. Track best_hard.
- Gradient via full soft weights.
- Update z with adaptiveline search: trial z, compute c_t, Fsmooth_t, maybe hard_t. Accept if Fsmooth_t <= Fsmooth + 1e-12. If accepted and hard_t < best_hard update. Else reject and reduce lr.
This is consistent and stable. At very small τ, smooth ≈ hard + τ log count; minimizing smooth effectively minimizes hard, with bias to reduce number of active peaks? τ log count may favor fewer active peaks but small.

Since τ changes over time, line search objective changes between iterations. That's okay if τ decreases slowly.

Full soft gradient details:
```
def full_gradient_weights(p, c, tau, n):
    M=c.max()
    if tau <= 0 or M <=0:
       top = np.argmax(c); w=np.zeros_like(c); w[top]=1.0
    else:
       ex=(c-M)/tau
       # ex <=0, clip lower -50
       ex = np.where(ex < -50, -50, ex)
       w=np.exp(ex)
       s=w.sum()
       if not finite or s==0: w...
       w/=s
    # convolve p and w_rev
    w_rev=w[::-1]
    size = len(p)+len(w_rev)-1 = 3n-2
    fsize = 1 << (size-1).bit_length()
    P=fft(p,fsize); W=fft(w_rev,fsize)
    h=np.real(ifft(P*W))[:size]
    # m = h[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]
    m = h[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]
    grad = 4.0*n*m
    return grad, M
```

Let's implement top loop efficiently:
```
def top_gradient(p, c, K, tau, n):
    L=len(c)
    if K >= L: top=np.arange(L)
    else:
       # argpartition may be slower but okay
       top = np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:]
    top_c = c[top]
    if tau <= 0:
        # weights uniform over top_c within max - active_tol
        tol = max(1e-12, 1e-7*M)
        active = top_c >= M - tol
        if not np.any(active): active = top_c >= np.max(top_c) - 1e-15
        w = np.zeros(K); w[active]=1/active.sum()
    else:
        ex=(top_c-M)/tau
        ex=np.clip(ex,-40,0)
        w=np.exp(ex); s=w.sum(); w = w/s if s>0 else ...
    grad=np.zeros(n)
    for idx in range(K):
        wk=w[idx]
        if wk <= 1e-15: continue
        k=int(top[idx])
        lo = k-(n-1)
        if lo < 0: lo=0
        hi = k if k < n else n-1
        # p slice:
        grad[lo:hi+1] += wk * 2.0 * p[k-hi:k-lo+1][::-1]
    grad *= 2.0 * n
    return grad, M
```
`np.argpartition` each iter may be nontrivial. We can use `top = c.argsort()[::-1][:K]`? Sorting 16k each iter O(n log n) slower. Argpartition O(n). Good.

If tau very small, weights maybe only max. If multiple top within K and ex=0 for maxima, uniform among maxima. Good.

Pseudo for run:
```
def run_opt(p0,n,budget,rng,opt):
   p=normalize_p(p0)
   z=log(p+1e-300)-mean
   c=fft_conv_self(p)
   F=2n*c.max()
   best=F; best_p=p.copy()
   v=zeros
   lr=opt.lr
   no_imp=0; iter=0
   start=time.monotonic(); total=budget
   while time-start < total:
       progress=(time-start)/total
       tau = opt.start_tau + (opt.end_tau-opt.start_tau)*progress
       K = int(opt.start_K + (opt.end_K-opt.start_K)*progress)
       grad, M = compute_gradient(...)
       F=2n*M
       if F < best* (1-1e-9): best=F; best_p=p.copy(); no_imp=0
       else: no_imp +=1
       gmax=abs(grad).max(); if gmax ==0: break
       step=grad/gmax
       step -= step.mean()
       step = np.clip(step, -opt.step_cap, opt.step_cap)
       if opt.momentum: v = opt.beta*v + (1-opt.beta)*step
       else: v=step
       # line search
       trial_lr=lr
       accepted=False
       for _ in range(3):
          zt=z-trial_lr*v
          zt-=zt.mean()
          pt=softmax(zt)
          ct=fft_conv_self(pt)
          Ft=2n*ct.max()
          if finite and Ft <= F + opt.tol:
              z=zt; p=pt; c=ct; F=Ft
              accepted=True
              if Ft < F_old - tol2: lr=min(max, lr*1.1)
              else: lr=max(min, lr*0.8)
              break
          trial_lr *=0.5
       if not accepted:
          lr=max(min, lr*0.5)
          if lr < min and no_imp>...:
             # perturb
          no_imp_found? If reject many, maybe small perturb to escape.
       iter +=1
       if no_imp > opt.stall and rng.random()<opt.perturb_prob:
           # add noise
           noise = opt.start_noise*(1-progress)+opt.end_noise*progress
           z += rng.normal(0,noise,n); z-=mean; p=softmax(z); c=fft; F=...
           # do not necessarily update best; if F much worse, maybe revert? To escape, accept but record? We can accept if F <= best*(1+0.05) or always from current.
           if not finite or F > best*1.1: # revert noise
              z=... from p? keep old by restoring? We need saved old.
```

Barker sequences have low aperiodic autocorrelation of s with itself, i.e. ∑ s_i s_{i+d} small. But our Q_k = ∑_{i+j=k} s_i s_j is convolution of s with itself, not autocorrelation. Function `make_p_from_delta(delta, n)`:
```
m=n//2
p=np.empty(n)
p[:m] = (1.0 + delta)/n
p[n-1:m-1:-1]? Need second half: for i in 0..m-1 rev=n-1-i value (1-delta[i])/n.
for i in range(m): p[n-1-i] = (1.0 - delta[i])/n
if n odd: p[m]=1.0/n? Wait if n odd, middle pairs? For odd n, pair sum condition for i<m (floor n/2) and middle p_mid =? Sum of m pairs 2/n plus middle? Total m*2/n + p_mid =1 => p_mid=1 - 2m/n =1/n. For n=2m+1, p_mid=1/n. Good.
```
Check for n even: m=n/2, p[:m]=..., p[n-1:n-m-1:-1] assignment easier:
```
rev_idx = np.arange(n-1, m-1, -1) # length m? For n=8,m=4, rev_idx [7,6,5]
p[rev_idx] = (1 - delta)/n
```
Actually pair for delta[0] with index n-1; delta[1] with n-2. So `p[:m]=(1+delta)/n; p[n-1:n-m-1:-1]=(1-delta)/n`. For n=8,m=4, n-m-1=3, slice 7:3:-1 gives indices 7,6,5 correct. Good. For odd n=7,m=3, slice [6,5,4], middle index3 set 1/n.

If delta values beyond [-1,1] clip.

Initial delta from p:
```
m=n//2
diff = p[:m] - p[n-1:n-m-1:-1]  # p_i - p_rev
delta = n * diff / 2.0
delta = clip(delta, -1,1)
```
This extracts antisymmetric part. If original p has symmetric deviations, discarded.

Gradient gdelta from g_p:
```
g_rev = (g[n-1:n-m-1:-1])  # aligns with delta indices
gdelta = (g[:m] - g_rev)/float(n)
```

Line search in δ. Since δ is dimension m (4096 for n=8192), vector operations cheaper. Need ensure p nonnegative. We can use box projection.

Gradient normalization: gdelta components raw maybe O(1/n). Normalize by max abs. If max zero, break.

Step size lr in δ space. Use backtracking. Because δ in [-1,1], step 0.2 good. At boundaries, clip.

Temperature/weights same on c. K maybe 16-64.

Could also optimize δ with Adam? Projected plain line search may be enough. We can add momentum: v = beta*v + (1-beta)*step; trial = delta - lr * v/clipped? But v may exceed. Use no momentum or beta 0.5.

Let's outline `run_delta_optimizer(delta0, n, budget, rng, params)`:
- delta = clip(delta0)
- p=make_p; c=fft; F=2n max
- best=F, best_delta=delta.copy()
- lr=params.lr (maybe 0.3), v=zeros
- start_time, total budget
- progress schedule tau, K, beta, lr_max etc.
- loop until time:
   tau = start_tau + (end_tau-start_tau)*progress (relative M)
   K = int(round(start_K + (end_K-start_K)*progress))
   grad_p, M = top_gradient(p,c,K,tau,n)
   F=2n*M; update best
   gdelta = (grad_p[:m] - gdelta_rev)/n
   gmax=abs(gdelta).max(); if gmax==0 break
   step = gdelta/gmax; step -=? no mean; clip [-1,1]
   if num_iterations>... use momentum beta
   trial_lr=lr; accept=False
   for r in range(3):
       d_trial = delta - trial_lr * step_eff
       d_trial = clip(d_trial, -1,1)
       p_trial = make_p(d_trial)
       c_trial = fft; Ft=...
       if Ft <= F + tol: accept; if Ft < F - dec: lr=min(max,lr*1.05) else lr*=0.95; break
       trial_lr*=0.5
   if not accept:
       lr=max(min,lr*0.5)
       if many fails: maybe perturb delta with random noise
   else: delta=d_trial; p=c=F
   iterations++
   if no improvement for stall: add small noise scaled by (1-progress) to delta, clip; recompute p,c,F (accept if not too bad? For escape, always accept if finite)
- return best_delta, best_p, best_F

```
def generate_deltas(n, rng, prev_a):
    inits=[]
    m=n//2
    if prev_a is not None:
        p=normalize_p(prev_a)
        d=delta_from_p(p,n); inits.append(('prev',d))
        for noise in (0.05,0.1,0.2):
            p2 = p + rng.normal(0, noise/n, n); p2=np.clip(p2,0,1000); p2/=p2.sum()
            inits.append(('prev_noise_%s'%noise, delta_from_p(p2,n)))
    # sine profiles
    x=np.linspace(0,1,m,endpoint=False)
    for amp in (0.3,0.5,0.7,0.9):
      for freq in (1,2,3,5):
        d=amp*np.sin(2*np.pi*freq*x + 0.0)
        inits.append(('sine_%d_%d'%(amp*10,freq), clip(d)))
      # cosine? antisym requires g(0)=? sin gives zero at 0; cos? For δ first half, no endpoint constraint. Try cos(freq*pi*x)
    # random Gaussian
    for sd in (0.3,0.6,1.0): d=clip(rng.normal(0,sd,m))
    # block step: first q fraction high
    for q in (0.25,0.4,0.5,0.6):
       d=np.full(m,0.0); d[:int(q*m)]=0.5 or 1? Use amplitude 0.5.
    return list
```

Let's detail `run_delta`:
```
def run_delta(delta0, n, max_time, rng, start_tau=0.08, end_tau=0.004, start_K=12, end_K=48, lr_init=0.6, lr_min=1e-4, lr_max=2.0, start_beta=0.0, end_beta=0.6, tol=1e-10):
    if n < 4: return
    m=n//2
    delta=np.clip(np.asarray(delta0,dtype=np.float64), -1,1)
    if delta.shape[0] != m: resize... 
    p=make_p_delta(delta,n)
    c=fft_conv_self(p)
    F=2n*c.max()
    bestF=F; bestDelta=delta.copy(); bestP=p.copy()
    v=np.zeros(m)
    lr=lr_init
    start=time.monotonic(); end=start+max_time
    last_improve=start
    stall_count=0
    iter=0
    while time.monotonic() < end - 0.5:
       progress=min(1.0, (time.monotonic()-start)/max(1e-6,max_time))
       tau = start_tau + (end_tau-start_tau)*progress
       K = int(round(start_K + (end_K-start_K)*progress))
       beta = start_beta + (end_beta-start_beta)*progress
       grad, M = top_gradient(p,c,min(K, len(c)), tau, n)
       F=2n*M
       if F < bestF - 1e-12:
           bestF=F; bestDelta=delta.copy(); bestP=p.copy(); last_improve=time.monotonic()
       gdel=(grad[:m] - grad[n-1:n-m-1:-1]) / float(n)
       gmax=np.max(np.abs(gdel))
       if not finite or gmax <= 1e-14: break
       step=gdel/gmax
       # maybe center? no
       if beta > 0:
          v = beta*v + (1-beta)*step
          if beta > 1e-6:
             v = v / max(1.0, 1.0/(1.0-beta)?) No, if v magnitude grows? We can just use v as is; initial zero, converges to step. Good.
       else: v=step
       v = np.clip(v, -5,5)
       # line search
       trial_lr=lr
       accepted=False
       F_old=F
       for rep in range(3):
          dt = delta - trial_lr * v
          dt = np.clip(dt, -1,1)
          pt=make_p_delta(dt,n)
          ct=fft_conv_self(pt)
          Ft=float(2.0*n*np.max(ct))
          if np.isfinite(Ft) and Ft <= F_old + tol:
              delta=dt; p=pt; c=ct; F=Ft; accepted=True
              if Ft < F_old - 1e-8:
                  lr = min(lr_max, lr*1.15)
              else:
                  lr = max(lr_min, lr*0.85)
              break
          trial_lr *= 0.5
       if not accepted:
          lr = max(lr_min, lr*0.5)
          # if very small and stuck, noise
          if lr <= lr_min*1.2 and time.monotonic() - last_improve > 30:
              noise = 0.08*(1.0-progress)+0.01
              delta = np.clip(delta + rng.normal(0, noise, m), -1,1)
              p=make_p_delta(delta,n); c=fft_conv_self(p); F=2n*c.max()
              if F < bestF: update
              # maybe reset lr
              lr = min(lr_max, lr*5)
       iter +=1
       if iter % 1000 ==0: print(...)
    return bestDelta,bestP,bestF
```

```
  p=normalize_p(p0); p=np.maximum(p,1e-300); p/=p.sum()
  z=np.log(p); z-=z.mean()
  c=fft... F=...
  best...
  lr=...
  v=zeros(n)
  while time<end:
    grad,M=top_gradient(...)

    update
    step=grad/max; step-=mean; clip
    beta progress; v...
    line search z: zt=z - trial_lr*v; zt-=mean; pt=softmax; Ft
    accept...
  return best_p,bestF
```

```
Full FFT gradient function:
```
def full_gradient_from_weights(p, weights, n):
   size = n + len(weights) - 1
   fsize = 1 << (size-1).bit_length()
   W=fft(weights[::-1], fsize)
   P=fft(p, fsize)
   h=np.real(ifft(P*W))[:size]
   m = h[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]
   return 4.0*n*m

def normalize_p(a, n=None):
    arr=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    arr=np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr=np.clip(arr,0.0,1000.0)
    if n is not None:
       if arr.size != n:
          # resize by simple linear interpolation? If n same? If arr larger truncate? If smaller pad.
          if arr.size ==0: arr=np.ones(n)/n
          else:
             new=np.empty(n); idx=np.linspace(0,arr.size-1,n); idx0=np.floor(idx).astype(int); idx1=np.minimum(idx0+1,arr.size-1); frac=idx-idx0; new=arr[idx0]*(1-frac)+arr[idx1]*frac; arr=new
    s=arr.sum()
    if s <= 1e-15: return np.full(arr.size, 1.0/arr.size)
    return arr/s

```
Detailed code:

```
import numpy as np
import time
from scipy.fft import fft, ifft

def _clip_finite(arr):
    arr = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64)
    arr[~np.isfinite(arr)] = 0.0
    return arr
```

`normalize_p`:
```
def normalize_p(a, n=None):
    arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if n is not None and arr.size != n:
        if arr.size == 0:
            arr = np.zeros(n)
        elif arr.size < n:
            idx = np.linspace(0, arr.size - 1, n)
            i0 = np.floor(idx).astype(np.int64)
            i1 = np.minimum(i0 + 1, arr.size - 1)
            fr = idx - i0
            arr = arr[i0] * (1.0 - fr) + arr[i1] * fr
        else:
            idx = np.linspace(0, arr.size - 1, n)
            i0 = np.floor(idx).astype(np.int64)
            i1 = np.minimum(i0 + 1, arr.size - 1)
            fr = idx - i0
            arr = arr[i0] * (1.0 - fr) + arr[i1] * fr
    if arr.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1)
    s = float(arr.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(arr.size, 1.0 / float(arr.size))
    return arr / s
```
If n=8192 and arr empty, arr zeros then s=0 -> full ones. Good.

`fft_conv_self`:
```
def fft_conv_self(a):
    n = len(a)
    size = 2*n - 1
    fsize = 1 << (size - 1).bit_length()
    A = fft(a, fsize)
    c = ifft(A * A).real
    if c.size > size: c = c[:size]
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return c
```
For n=1? fsize bit of 0? n at least 8192. okay.

`hard_objective`:
```
def hard_objective(c,n):
    if c.size ==0: return np.inf
    mx=float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(mx) or mx<0: return np.inf
    return float(2.0*float(n)*mx)
```

`top_gradient`:
```
def top_gradient(p,c,K,tau_frac,n):
    L = len(c)
    M = float(np.max(c)) if L else 0.0
    if not np.isfinite(M) or M <= 0:
        return np.zeros(n), M
    K = int(max(1, min(K, L)))
    if K == L:
        idx = np.arange(L)
    else:
        idx = np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:]
    top_c = c[idx]
    tau = max(1e-300, float(tau_frac) * M)
    ex = (top_c - M) / tau
    ex = np.where(np.isfinite(ex), ex, -40.0)
    ex = np.clip(ex, -40.0, 0.0)
    w = np.exp(ex)
    s = float(np.sum(w))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        w = np.zeros(K)
        w[int(np.argmax(top_c))] = 1.0
    else:
        w /= s
    grad = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    # local variables for speed
    pn = int(n)
    for ii in range(K):
        wk = float(w[ii])
        if wk <= 1e-15: continue
        k = int(idx[ii])
        lo = k - (pn - 1)
        if lo < 0: lo = 0
        hi = k if k < pn else pn - 1
        # slice start = k-hi, end = k-lo+1
        grad[lo:hi+1] += wk * 2.0 * p[k-hi:k-lo+1][::-1]
    grad *= float(2.0 * pn)
    return grad, M
```
`np.argmax(top_c)` works. If K=1, okay.

`make_p_delta`:
```
def make_p_delta(delta, n):
    m = n // 2
    d = np.asarray(delta, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if d.size < m:
        d = np.pad(d, (0, m - d.size), mode='constant')
    else:
        d = d[:m]
    d = np.clip(d, -1.0, 1.0)
    p = np.full(n, 1.0 / float(n), dtype=np.float64)
    if m > 0:
        p[:m] = (1.0 + d) / float(n)
        p[n-1:n-m-1:-1] = (1.0 - d) / float(n)
    if n % 2 == 1:
        p[m] = 1.0 / float(n)
    s = p.sum()
    if s <= 1e-300: return np.full(n,1.0/n)
    # sum should be 1, but normalize for safety
    p = p / s
    return p
```
For even, p sum: m*(2)/n =1. Good. Due clip, sum still 1? Each pair sums 2/n, yes. Floating normalization okay. For odd, pairs m*2/n + 1/n = (2m+1)/n=1.

`delta_from_p`:
```
def delta_from_p(p,n):
    m=n//2
    if m ==0: return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.asarray(p,dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if p.size != n: p=normalize_p(p,n)? But avoid recursion; can interpolate? Assume.
    left = p[:m]
    right = p[n-1:n-m-1:-1]
    d = (float(n) * (left - right)) / 2.0
    return np.clip(d, -1.0, 1.0)
```
If p size mismatch, not.

`project_antisym`:
```
def project_antisym(p,n):
    return make_p_delta(delta_from_p(p,n), n)
```

`softmax_z`:
```
def softmax_z(z):
    z = np.asarray(z,dtype=np.float64)
    z = z - float(np.max(z))
    z = np.clip(z, -700.0, 0.0)
    e = np.exp(z)
    s = float(np.sum(e))
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones_like(z)/z.size
    return e/s

```
def make_initializations(n,rng,prev_p):
   m=n//2; inits=[]
   d0=delta_from_p(prev_p,n); inits.append(('prev', d0))
   for idx, ns in enumerate((0.03,0.08,0.18)):
       p2=prev_p + rng.normal(0.0, ns/float(n), n)
       p2=np.maximum(p2,0.0); s=p2.sum()
       if s>0: p2=p2/s
       else: p2=prev_p
       inits.append(('prevn%d' % idx, delta_from_p(p2,n)))
   x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,m,endpoint=False)
   for idx,(f,amp) in enumerate([(1,0.7),(2,0.7),(3,0.9),(5,0.8)]):
       d=amp*np.sin(2.0*np.pi*f*x + 0.2*idx)
       inits.append(('sin%d'%idx, np.clip(d,-1,1)))
   # random smooth
   d = rng.normal(0.0,0.7,m)
   inits.append(('rand0', np.clip(d,-1,1)))
   d = rng.normal(0.0,0.4,m)
   inits.append(('rand1', np.clip(d,-1,1)))
   # block
   d=np.zeros(m); d[:m//2]=0.5; inits.append(('blk0',d))
   d=np.zeros(m); d[:m//4]=-0.4; d[m//4:m//2]=0.6; inits.append(('blk1',d))
   return inits
```
Maybe 11 inits too many; phase1 per smaller. Limit to e.g 8: prev, prevn1, sin1, sin2, rand0, blk0, sin3, prevn2. We can slice after.

Now, evaluate sequence function: We assume `evaluate_sequence` in global. To scale p:
```
def scaled_output(p,n):
   p=np.asarray(p,dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
   p=np.where(np.isfinite(p),p,0); p=np.clip(p,0,1000)
   s=p.sum()
   if s <=1e-300: return np.ones(n)/n*np.sqrt(2n)
   target=np.sqrt(2.0*float(n))
   return p/s*target
```

Propose flow detailed:
```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2380):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    start=time.monotonic()
    deadline=start + max(120.0, float(budget_s)-40.0)
    n=8192
    prev_p=None
    try:
       prev_p=normalize_p(height_sequence_1,n)
    except Exception:
       prev_p=None
    if prev_p is None or not np.all(np.isfinite(prev_p)):
       prev_p=np.ones(n)/n
    # maybe verify prev not too slow? no
    c=fft_conv_self(prev_p); global_F=hard_objective(c,n); global_p=prev_p.copy()
    print("initial F=%.6f" % global_F)
    inits=make_initializations(n,rng,prev_p)
    # optionally quick evaluate initial F to order? We can just run.
    basins=[]
    # phase1
```

```python
"""
Search for non-negative step-function heights minimizing

    2*n*max(conv(a,a)) / (sum(a)**2)

by working in the scale-invariant probability simplex (sum p = 1).  The main
refinement is performed in an antisymmetric subspace p_i + p_{n-1-i} = 2/n,
which is a strong structural ansatz for this non-negative convolution
problem.  A conservative unconstrained log-space mirror search is also used
on the best point.  The search uses FFT self-convolutions, smoothed top-lag
subgradients, backtracking line searches, and several diverse initial
references.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
from scipy.fft import fft, ifft


def normalize_p(a, n=None):
    """Non-negative float vector normalized to sum 1, optionally length n."""
    arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)

    if n is not None and arr.size != n:
        if arr.size == 0:
            arr = np.zeros(max(1, int(n)), dtype=np.float64)
        else:
            idx = np.linspace(0.0, arr.size - 1, int(n))
            i0 = np.floor(idx).astype(np.int64)
            i1 = np.minimum(i0 + 1, arr.size - 1)
            fr = idx - i0
            arr = arr[i0] * (1.0 - fr) + arr[i1] * fr

    if arr.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)

    s = float(arr.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(arr.size, 1.0 / float(arr.size), dtype=np.float64)
    return arr / s


def fft_conv_self(a):
    """Linear self-convolution a*a, length 2n-1, using FFT."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    size = 2 * n - 1
    fsize = 1 << max(1, (size - 1).bit_length())
    A = fft(a, fsize)
    c = ifft(A * A).real
    if c.size > size:
        c = c[:size]
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return c


def hard_objective(c, n):
    """Return 2*n*max(c), the objective for p with sum 1."""
    if c.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    mx = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(mx) or mx < 0.0:
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * float(n) * mx)


def top_gradient(p, c, K, tau_frac, n):
    """
    Smoothed subgradient of max(c) using the K largest lags.

    Weights are softmax((c_M - c_k)/tau) among those K lags.  The returned
    gradient is d(2n*max(c))/dp, scaled, but later normalized by the caller.
    """
    L = int(c.size)
    if L == 0:
        return np.zeros(int(n), dtype=np.float64), 0.0

    M = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(M) or M <= 0.0:
        return np.zeros(int(n), dtype=np.float64), M

    K = int(max(1, min(K, L)))
    if K == L:
        idx = np.arange(L, dtype=np.int64)
    else:
        idx = np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:]

    top_c = c[idx]
    tau = max(1e-300, float(tau_frac) * M)
    ex = (top_c - M) / tau
    ex = np.where(np.isfinite(ex), ex, -40.0)
    ex = np.clip(ex, -40.0, 0.0)
    w = np.exp(ex)
    ws = float(np.sum(w))
    if not np.isfinite(ws) or ws <= 0.0:
        w = np.zeros(K, dtype=np.float64)
        w[int(np.argmax(top_c))] = 1.0
    else:
        w /= ws

    grad = np.zeros(int(n), dtype=np.float64)
    pn = int(n)

    for ii in range(K):
        wk = float(w[ii])
        if wk <= 1e-15:
            continue
        k = int(idx[ii])
        lo = k - (pn - 1)
        if lo < 0:
            lo = 0
        hi = k if k < pn else pn - 1
        # For lag k: d c_k / d p_i = 2 p_{k-i}, for i in [lo, hi].
        grad[lo:hi + 1] += wk * 2.0 * p[k - hi:k - lo + 1][::-1]

    grad *= float(2.0 * pn)
    return grad, M


def make_p_delta(delta, n):
    """
    Construct p in the antisymmetric subspace:
        p_i = (1 + delta_i)/n,
        p_{n-1-i} = (1 - delta_i)/n,
    for i < n/2.  For odd n the middle coordinate is 1/n.
    """
    n = int(n)
    m = n // 2
    d = np.asarray(delta, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if d.size < m:
        d = np.pad(d, (0, m - d.size), mode="constant", constant_values=0.0)
    else:
        d = d[:m].copy()
    d = np.clip(d, -1.0, 1.0)

    p = np.full(n, 1.0 / float(n), dtype=np.float64)
    if m > 0:
        p[:m] = (1.0 + d) / float(n)
        p[n - 1:n - m - 1:-1] = (1.0 - d) / float(n)
    if n % 2 == 1:
        p[m] = 1.0 / float(n)

    s = float(p.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(n, 1.0 / float(n), dtype=np.float64)
    return p / s


def delta_from_p(p, n):
    """Extract the antisymmetric part delta_i = n/2 * (p_i - p_{n-1-i})."""
    n = int(n)
    m = n // 2
    if m <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)

    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if p.size != n:
        p = normalize_p(p, n)

    left = p[:m]
    right = p[n - 1:n - m - 1:-1]
    d = (float(n) * (left - right)) / 2.0
    return np.clip(d, -1.0, 1.0)


def project_antisym(p, n):
    """Project a probability vector to the p_i + p_{n-1-i}=2/n subspace."""
    return make_p_delta(delta_from_p(p, n), n)


def softmax_z(z):
    """Stable softmax from a log-space vector."""
    z = np.asarray(z, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if z.size == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    z = z - float(np.max(z))
    z = np.clip(z, -700.0, 0.0)
    e = np.exp(z)
    s = float(np.sum(e))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones_like(z) / float(z.size)
    return e / s


def delta_params(phase):
    """Parameters for the antisymmetric delta optimizer."""
    if phase == "explore":
        # start_tau, end_tau, start_K, end_K,
        # lr_init, lr_max, lr_min, beta_start, beta_end
        return 0.10, 0.0045, 12, 48, 0.70, 2.0, 1e-5, 0.0, 0.54
    if phase == "polish":
        return 0.011, 0.00052, 24, 64, 0.34, 1.0, 1e-5, 0.32, 0.72
    if phase == "final":
        return 0.0028, 0.00009, 32, 64, 0.18, 0.55, 1e-6, 0.55, 0.82
    return 0.10, 0.0045, 12, 48, 0.70, 2.0, 1e-5, 0.0, 0.54


def mirror_params(phase):
    """Parameters for the unconstrained log-space mirror optimizer."""
    if phase == "explore":
        return 0.085, 0.005, 16, 48, 0.55, 1.6, 1e-5, 0.0, 0.45
    if phase == "polish":
        return 0.018, 0.0009, 24, 56, 0.32, 0.9, 1e-5, 0.30, 0.62
    return 0.018, 0.0009, 24, 56, 0.32, 0.9, 1e-5, 0.30, 0.62


def run_delta(delta0, n, max_time, rng, phase="explore"):
    """
    Optimize delta in [-1,1]^m inside the antisymmetric subspace.
    Returns (best_delta, best_p, best_F).
    """
    max_time = float(max(0.0, max_time))
    if max_time < 1.0:
        p = make_p_delta(delta0, n)
        c = fft_conv_self(p)
        F = hard_objective(c, n)
        return depclaude, p, F

    n = int(n)
    m = n // 2
    if m <= 0:
        p = normalize_p(np.ones(n))
        return delta0, p, 2.0

    estart, eend, km, kM, lr_init, lr_max, lr_min, beta_start, beta_end = delta_params(phase)

    delta = np.asarray(delta0, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1).copy()
    if delta.size < m:
        delta = np.pad(delta, (0, m - delta.size), mode="constant", constant_values=0.0)
    else:
        delta = delta[:m]
    delta = np.clip(delta, -1.0, 1.0)

    p = make_p_delta(delta, n)
    c = fft_conv_self(p)
    F = hard_objective(c, n)
    if not np.isfinite(F):
        p = np.full(n, 1.0 / float(n))
        c = fft_conv_self(p)
        F = 2.0

    bestF = F
    bestDelta = delta.copy()
    bestP = p.copy()

    v = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
    lr = max(lr_min, min(lr_max, lr_init))

    start = time.monotonic()
    end = start + max_time
    last_improve = start
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < end - 0.30:
        prog = min(1.0, (time.monotonic() - start) / max(1e-9, max_time))
        tau = estart + (eend - estart) * prog
        K = int(round(km + (kM - km) * prog))
        beta = beta_start + (beta_end - beta_start) * prog

        grad, M = top_gradient(p, c, K, tau, n)
        F = max(0.0, float(2.0 * n * M))

        if np.isfinite(F) and F < bestF - 1e-12:
            bestF = F
            bestDelta = delta.copy()
            bestP = p.copy()
            last_improve = time.monotonic()

        gm = float(np.max(np.abs(grad)))
        if not np.isfinite(gm) or gm <= 1e-16:
            break

        gdelta = (grad[:m] - grad[n - 1:n - m - 1:-1]) / float(n)
        gdmax = float(np.max(np.abs(gdelta)))
        if not np.isfinite(gdmax) or gdmax <= 1e-15:
            break

        step = gdelta / gdmax
        if beta > 1e-8:
            v = beta * v + (1.0 - beta) * step
        else:
            v = step.copy()
        v = np.clip(v, -5.0, 5.0)

        F_old = F
        accepted = False

        for factor in (1.0, 0.5, 0.25):
            trial_lr = lr * factor
            if trial_lr <= 0.0:
                continue
            dt = delta - trial_lr * v
            dt = np.clip(dt, -1.0, 1.0)
            if float(np.max(np.abs(dt - delta))) <= 1e-14:
                continue

            pt = make_p_delta(dt, n)
            ct = fft_conv_self(pt)
            Ft = hard_objective(ct, n)

            if np.isfinite(Ft) and Ft <= F_old + 1e-10:
                delta = dt
                p = pt
                c = ct
                F = Ft
                accepted = True
                if Ft < F_old - 1e-9:
                    lr = min(lr_max, max(lr * 1.08, trial_lr * 1.5))
                else:
                    lr = max(lr_min, trial_lr * 0.8)
                break

        if not accepted:
            lr = max(lr_min, lr * 0.5)

            # If we have drifted badly from the locally best point, reset and
            # try a small perturbation to escape a bad basin.
            if bestF < np.inf and F > bestF * 1.06:
                delta = bestDelta.copy()
                p = bestP.copy()
                c = fft_conv_self(p)
                F = max(0.0, bestF)
                v.fill(0.0)
                lr = max(lr, 0.18)

            if time.monotonic() - last_improve > max(22.0, 0.025 * max_time):
                if rng.random() < 0.012:
                    noise_scale = 0.085 * (1.0 - prog) + 0.012
                    old_delta = delta.copy()
                    old_p = p.copy()
                    old_c = c.copy()
                    old_F = F

                    delta = delta + rng.normal(0.0, noise_scale, m)
                    delta = np.clip(delta, -1.0, 1.0)
                    p = make_p_delta(delta, n)
                    c = fft_conv_self(p)
                    F = hard_objective(c, n)

                    if np.isfinite(F) and F < max(old_F, bestF) * 1.035 + 1e-8:
                        v.fill(0.0)
                        lr = max(lr, 0.22)
                    else:
                        delta = old_delta
                        p = old_p
                        c = old_c
                        F = old_F

        it += 1
        if it % 7000 == 0:
            print("[delta %s] iter=%d F=%.6f bestF=%.6f lr=%.4f tau=%.5f K=%d"
                  % (phase, it, F, bestF, lr, tau, K), flush=True)

    return bestDelta, bestP, bestF


def run_mirror(p0, n, max_time, rng, phase="polish"):
    """
    Unconstrained log-space mirror line-search on the simplex.
    Returns (best_p, best_F).
    """
    max_time = float(max(0.0, max_time))
    if max_time < 1.0:
        p = normalize_p(p0, n)
        c = fft_conv_self(p)
        F = hard_objective(c, n)
        return p, F

    n = int(n)
    estart, eend, km, kM, lr_init, lr_max, lr_min, beta_start, beta_end = mirror_params(phase)

    p = normalize_p(p0, n)
    p = np.maximum(p, 1e-300)
    p = p / float(p.sum())
    z = np.log(p)
    z = z - float(np.mean(z))

    c = fft_conv_self(p)
    F = hard_objective(c, n)
    if not np.isfinite(F):
        p = np.full(n, 1.0 / float(n))
        z = np.zeros(n)
        c = fft_conv_self(p)
        F = 2.0

    bestF = F
    bestP = p.copy()

    v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    lr = max(lr_min, min(lr_max, lr_init))

    start = time.monotonic()
    end = start + max_time
    last_improve = start
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < end - 0.30:
        prog = min(1.0, (time.monotonic() - start) / max(1e-9, max_time))
        tau = estart + (eend - estart) * prog
        K = int(round(km + (kM - km) * prog))
        beta = beta_start + (beta_end - beta_start) * prog

        grad, M = top_gradient(p, c, K, tau, n)
        F = max(0.0, float(2.0 * n * M))

        if np.isfinite(F) and F < bestF - 1e-12:
            bestF = F
            bestP = p.copy()
            last_improve = time.monotonic()

        gm = float(np.max(np.abs(grad)))
        if not np.isfinite(gm) or gm <= 1e-16:
            break

        step = grad / gm
        step = step - float(np.mean(step))
        step = np.clip(step, -3.0, 3.0)

        if beta > 1e-8:
            v = beta * v + (1.0 - beta) * step
        else:
            v = step.copy()
        v = np.clip(v, -3.0, 3.0)

        F_old = F
        accepted = False

        for factor in (1.0, 0.5, 0.25):
            trial_lr = lr * factor
            if trial_lr <= 0.0:
                continue
            zt = z - trial_lr * v
            zt = zt - float(np.mean(zt))
            pt = softmax_z(zt)
            if float(np.max(np.abs(pt - p))) <= 1e-14:
                continue

            ct = fft_conv_self(pt)
            Ft = hard_objective(ct, n)

            if np.isfinite(Ft) and Ft <= F_old + 1e-10:
                z = zt
                p = pt
                c = ct
                F = Ft
                accepted = True
                if Ft < F_old - 1e-9:
                    lr = min(lr_max, max(lr * 1.08, trial_lr * 1.5))
                else:
                    lr = max(lr_min, trial_lr * 0.8)
                break

        if not accepted:
            lr = max(lr_min, lr * 0.5)

            if bestF < np.inf and F > bestF * 1.06:
                p = bestP.copy()
                z = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-300))
                z = z - float(np.mean(z))
                c = fft_conv_self(p)
                F = max(0.0, bestF)
                v.fill(0.0)
                lr = max(lr, 0.15)

            if time.monotonic() - last_improve > max(22.0, 0.025 * max_time):
                if rng.random() < 0.012:
                    noise_scale = 0.12 * (1.0 - prog) + 0.02
                    old_z = z.copy()
                    old_p = p.copy()
                    old_c = c.copy()
                    old_F = F

                    z = z + rng.normal(0.0, noise_scale, n)
                    z = z - float(np.mean(z))
                    p = softmax_z(z)
                    c = fft_conv_self(p)
                    F = hard_objective(c, n)

                    if np.isfinite(F) and F < max(old_F, bestF) * 1.035 + 1e-8:
                        v.fill(0.0)
                        lr = max(lr, 0.2)
                    else:
                        z = old_z
                        p = old_p
                        c = old_c
                        F = old_F

        it += 1
        if it % 7000 == 0:
            print("[mirror %s] iter=%d F=%.6f bestF=%.6f lr=%.4f tau=%.5f K=%d"
                  % (phase, it, F, bestF, lr, tau, K), flush=True)

    return bestP, bestF


def make_initializations(n, rng, prev_p):
    """Generate a diversified set of antisymmetric initial delta vectors."""
    m = n // 2
    inits = []

    d0 = delta_from_p(prev_p, n)
    inits.append(("prev", d0.copy()))

    p = prev_p
    for j, ns in enumerate((0.035, 0.090, 0.200)):
        p2 = p + rng.normal(0.0, ns / float(n), n)
        p2 = np.maximum(p2, 0.0)
        s2 = float(p2.sum())
        if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 > 1e-300:
            p2 = p2 / s2
        else:
            p2 = p
        inits.append(("prevn%d" % j, delta_from_p(p2, n)))

    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m, endpoint=False)

    # Low-frequency antisymmetric profiles.
    inits.append(("sin1", np.clip(0.70 * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * x), -1.0, 1.0)))
    inits.append(("sin2", np.clip(0.72 * np.sin(4.0 * np.pi * x + 0.35), -1.0, 1.0)))
    inits.append(("sin3", np.clip(0.86 * np.sin(6.0 * np.pi * x + 0.10), -1.0, 1.0)))
    inits.append(("lin1", np.clip(0.68 * (2.0 * x - 1.0), -1.0, 1.0)))

    # Smooth random profile.
    inits.append(("rand0", np.clip(rng.normal(0.0, 0.55, m), -1.0, 1.0)))

    # Two-block antisymmetric profile.
    dblk = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
    dblk[:m // 2] = 0.55
    inits.append(("blk0", dblk))

    # Three-orientation block profile.
    db2 = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
    db2[:m // 4] = -0.45
    db2[m // 4:m // 2] = 0.62
    db2[m // 2:3 * m // 4] = -0.22
    inits.append(("blk1", db2))

    # Keep a reasonable number.
    if len(inits) > 8:
        inits = inits[:8]

    return inits


def output_sequence(p, n):
    """Scale a sum-one probability vector to sum sqrt(2n), with safety clipping."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
    p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1000.0)

    if p.size != int(n):
        p = normalize_p(p, int(n))

    s = float(p.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        p = np.full(int(n), 1.0 / float(n), dtype=np.float64)
        s = 1.0

    target = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * float(n)))
    out = p / s * target
    out = np.clip(out, 0.0, 1000.0)

    if out.size == 0:
        out = np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    if float(out.sum()) < 0.01:
        out = np.full(int(n), target / float(max(1, int(n))), dtype=np.float64)

    return out


def verified_score(p, n):
    """Call the authoritative evaluator, if available, for a candidate vector."""
    try:
        out = output_sequence(p, n)
        val = evaluate_sequence(out.tolist())
        val = float(val)
        if not np.isfinite(val):
            return np.inf
        return val
    except Exception:
        return np.inf


def basin_score_key(x):
    return x[0]


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2380):
    """
    Main entry point. Search budget is internally managed to return well
    before the external 2400 second limit.
    """
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    internal_budget = max(120.0, float(budget_s) - 45.0)
    deadline = t0 + internal_budget

    n = 8192

    # Reference sequence, if supplied by the harness.
    prev_p = None
    try:
        prev_raw = height_sequence_1  # noqa: F821
        prev_p = normalize_p(prev_raw, n)
    except Exception:
        prev_p = None

    if prev_p is None or prev_p.size != n or not np.all(np.isfinite(prev_p)):
        prev_p = np.full(n, 1.0 / float(n), dtype=np.float64)

    c_prev = fft_conv_self(prev_p)
    global_F = hard_objective(c_prev, n)
    global_p = prev_p.copy()
    print("initial reference F=%.6f" % global_F, flush=True)

    inits = make_initializations(n, rng, prev_p)
    basins = []

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Phase 1: broad exploration from diverse antisymmetric references.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    n_inits = max(1, len(inits))
    phase1_total = min(0.34 * rem, float(n_inits) * 185.0)
    phase1_per = phase1_total / float(n_inits)
    phase1_per = min(175.0, max(28.0, phase1_per))

    for name, d in inits:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 950.0:
            break
        budget = min(phase1_per, deadline - time.monotonic())
        if budget < 18.0:
            break
        d, p, F = run_delta(d, n, budget, rng, phase="explore")
        basins.append((F, name, d, p))
        if np.isfinite(F) and F < global_F:
            global_F = F
            global_p = p
        print("phase1 %s F=%.6f global=%.6f t_left=%.1f"
              % (name, F, global_F, deadline - time.monotonic()), flush=True)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Phase 2: continue the most promising basins.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    if len(basins) > 1:
        basins.sort(key=basin_score_key)

    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    reserve_final = min(720.0, max(240.0, 0.42 * rem))
    usable = max(0.0, rem - reserve_final)

    if usable > 40.0 and basins:
        top = basins[:4]
        fracs = (0.42, 0.25, 0.18, 0.15)
        for rank in range(len(top)):
            if time.monotonic() > deadline - reserve_final:
                break
            budget = usable * fracs[rank]
            if budget < 22.0:
                continue
            F, name, d, p = top[rank]
            d, p, F2 = run_delta(d, n, budget, rng, phase="polish")
            if np.isfinite(F2) and F2 < global_F:
                global_F = F2
                global_p = p
            print("phase2 rank=%d %s F=%.6f global=%.6f t_left=%.1f"
                  % (rank, name, F2, global_F, deadline - time.monotonic()), flush=True)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Final polish: antisymmetric refinement, then a short unconstrained
    # log-space polish if time remains.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    left = deadline - time.monotonic() - 8.0
    if left > 35.0:
        d = delta_from_p(global_p, n)
        d, p, F = run_delta(d, n, 0.60 * left, rng, phase="final")
        if np.isfinite(F) and F < global_F:
            global_F = F
            global_p = p
        print("final delta F=%.6f global=%.6f t_left=%.1f"
              % (F, global_F, deadline - time.monotonic()), flush=True)

    left = deadline - time.monotonic() - 8.0
    if left > 70.0:
        p, F = run_mirror(global_p, n, 0.55 * left, rng, phase="polish")
        if np.isfinite(F) and F < global_F:
            global_F = F
            global_p = p
        print("final mirror F=%.6f global=%.6f t_left=%.1f"
              % (F, global_F, deadline - time.monotonic()), flush=True)

    # Try a last antisymmetric projection of whatever is currently best.
    p_proj = project_antisym(global_p, n)
    c_proj = fft_conv_self(p_proj)
    F_proj = hard_objective(c_proj, n)
    if np.isfinite(F_proj) and F_proj < global_F:
        global_F = F_proj
        global_p = p_proj
        print("projected antisym improves: F=%.6f" % global_F, flush=True)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Verify a few final candidates with the authoritative evaluator.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    best_p = global_p
    best_verified = np.inf

    candidates = [global_p, prev_p]
    # Add the last mirror point if it is distinct and present.
    try:
        if "p" in locals() and np.isfinite(F) and F <= global_F + 1e-9:
            candidates.append(p)
    except Exception:
        pass

    left = deadline - time.monotonic() - 3.0
    if left > 4.0:
        seen_shape = set()
        for cand in candidates:
            if time.monotonic() > deadline - 2.0:
                break
            try:
                cand = np.asarray(cand, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
                if cand.size == 0:
                    continue
                key = tuple(np.round(cand[:8], 6).tolist())
                if key in seen_shape:
                    continue
                seen_shape.add(key)
                val = verified_score(cand, n)
                if np.isfinite(val) and val < best_verified:
                    best_verified = val
                    best_p = cand
                print("verified cand F_ver=%.6f" % val, flush=True)
            except Exception:
                pass

    if not np.isfinite(best_verified):
        best_p = global_p

    out = output_sequence(best_p, n)
    if not all(np.isfinite(out)):
        out = np.full(n, float(np.sqrt(2.0 * float(n))) / float(n), dtype=np.float64)

    return [float(x) for x in out]
```
